fr3828_reading.md

# Richelieu to M. de Rancé (and to the Queen Mother), Feb–May 1629 (BnF fr. 3828, 3825, 3835): reading

<!-- measure: cipher_markup=⟦⟧ -->

Nine letters that Avenel prints with their cipher figures in place (Lettres de Richelieu III, 1858, nos. CXIX, CXXXV,
CXLVI, CXLVII, CLIII, CLIV, CLV, CLVIII, CLXVII), read with the fr. 3829 key (`key.md`, with its Nov 2026 extension).
Transcription: `fr3828_avenel_figures.txt`; unedited decryption: `fr3828_decryption.txt`; method and measure:
`fr3828_method.md`. The manuscripts were not seen: everything rests on the figures as Avenel printed them.

Conventions:

- The clear text is Avenel's, as transcribed (abridged with … away from the figures). Words written in cipher are
  between ⟦ ⟧, spelled as the figures spell them; `{...}` inside ⟦ ⟧ = a nomenclator figure, named by the key table.
- Spelling: word division, apostrophes, accents and capitals added; u/v and i/j written by sound (the key has 26 = u/v,
  21 = i/j). Nothing else is normalised: *efet*, *colation*, *Meaus*, *aus*, *afaire*, *aler* are the figures' spelling.
- `[?x]` = a word the figures as printed do not give; the reading follows Avenel's footnote; counted as a hole. The
  five cases: CXLVII *septembre* (last figure 29 = a: *septembra*); "le 20 mai" in CLIII, CLIV and CLV twice (20 is
  the key's d; Avenel takes it as the number); CLV *veuille* (figures *veule*); CLV *articles* (figures
  *articlesignes*, one s for *articles* and *signés*: 23 Nov 2026, review of #813); CLVIII *maistresse* (figures
  *mistrese*; Avenel notes the clerk left out the a).
- Code values taken from Avenel's note at that place rather than the key table (the 19 Nov 2026 rule counts them as
  context values): CLVIII 59 = *M. de Mantoue* twice (table: princesse Marie; Avenel: a slip of the secretary);
  CLIV 84 = *les Anglois* / *Angleterre* (table: l'Angleterre). They are written `{x}` and listed here. Under the strict
  count (owner's decision on #813, 19 Nov 2026) they are holes, like the ten values of the `key.md` Extension where
  they are read (CXIX 68, 69; CXLVI 69; CLIII 55, 61, 62, 95, 96, 97; CLXVII 41, 67, 68): see `fr3828_method.md`.
  The base codes 54 (Monsieur), 59 (princesse Marie) and 60 (Mme de Longueville), named by the key table, are holes
  too in the count (20 Nov 2026): their values come from Avenel's notes to these letters (`key.md`).
- `[d'une affaire]` in CLXVII is Avenel's own supply in the clear text ("il manque ici, dans le manuscrit, un mot").

## CXIX — fr. 3835 no. 15 (Béthune 9329 fol. 46), to Rancé, [15 (?) February 1629]

… Sa Majesté désire que la reyne luy die ceste nouvelle, et luy en baille le brevet ⟦tirant⟧ parole ⟦de
{le cardinal de Bérulle}⟧ qu'il ⟦n'unira⟧ aucun ⟦bénéfice de sa colation à sa congrégation⟧. ⟦{Le Roi}⟧ de son chef
désire cela.

Sa Majesté donne la Valasse à M. de Metz, et St-Faron de Meaux à M. de Moret. …

La lettre que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ a escrite ⟦au parlement⟧ est conçue en termes non bien choisis, à mon advis; je crains
que cela face ⟦un mauvais efet⟧ pour ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧, et il falloit mieux ménager ⟦son autorité et⟧ la bienveillance
de ⟦ceste compagnie vers {la Reine mère}⟧. N'en parlés qu'à ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧. ⟦{Le Roi}⟧ peut bien escrire
⟦ainsi⟧, mais il n'estoit pas à propos que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ ⟦en usast⟧ de la sorte.

Quant à ⟦Saint-Malo⟧, puisque ⟦{le garde des sceaux}⟧ le demande, je crois que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ luy doit accorder.
⟦{La Reine mère}⟧ me fait plus d'honneur que je ne vaux de désirer mon advis; ⟦la Racinière⟧, vieil serviteur,
mériteroit bien une pension de ⟦mil livres⟧.

⟦{La Reine mère}⟧ peut permettre à ⟦Madame de Vendosme⟧ d'aller ⟦coucher au bois de Vincenes⟧ plus souvent
puisqu'elle le désire.

## CXXXV — fr. 3825 no. 28 (Béthune 9319 fol. 77), to Rancé, Susa, 21 March [1629]

… J'ay trop de désir que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ soit adverty de tout ce qui se passe … Hier au soir ⟦{Mme de Longueville}⟧
receut un courrier, qui est venu à tue chevaux, qui dit que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ a fait mettre ⟦la {Mme de Longueville}
et {princesse Marie} au bois de Vincenes⟧. Nous attendons le boiteux pour croire ceste nouvelle … ⟦{La Reine mère}⟧
aura bien, je m'asseure, pourveu à faire ⟦adoucir l'esprit de {Monsieur}⟧ en mesme temps.

… Auparavant que sçavoir la nouvelle qu'a apporté le courrier de ⟦{Mme de Longueville}⟧, que nous ne croions pas
encore, j'estois en doute si ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ devoit faire chanter le Te Deum … pour estoufer par ceste joye les
appréhensions qu'on pourroit prendre ici ⟦mécontent⟧. Cependant, il faut parler des victoires du roy avec
⟦modestie⟧ pour ⟦ne piquer⟧ extraordinairement ⟦{l'Espagne}⟧.

De Suze, ce 21e mars.

## CXLVI — fr. 3828 no. 8 (Béthune 9322 fol. 24), to Rancé, 14 April 1629

Je suis extremement fasché du mal de teste de ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧. … L'honneur qu'il y a long temps que j'ay d'estre
⟦près d'ele⟧ me fait croire que les desplaisirs qu'il a de l'affaire de ⟦{Monsieur}⟧ sont cause en partie de son mal;
je voudrois de bon cœur que ceste affaire fust terminée au contentement de Lysandre, il faut tascher de la terminer;
⟦les gens de {Monsieur}⟧ verront bien maintenant que ⟦ce mariage⟧ n'est plus une affaire à laquelle il puisse penser,
et partant seront-ils plus faciles à ⟦le porter à⟧ donner contentement à ⟦{le Roi} et {la Reine mère}⟧.

Je ne sçavois point asseurément la grossesse de ⟦la {l'Angleterre}⟧. Si ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ y envoie, comme je croy
qu'elle le peust faire, il sera à propos d'y envoyer un homme ⟦capable d'afaires, d'agir et parler⟧. Je croy que le
sieur ⟦de Meaus⟧, qui y a desjà esté, y seroit plus propre qu'un autre. Aussy bien croy-je qu'il est à propos que
⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ attende jusqu'au commencement du mois prochain, auquel temps on sçaura la disposition qu'il y aura
pour ⟦la pais ou la guere⟧; et entre cy et là je vous envoieray une petite instruction pour ⟦ledit de Meaus⟧.

Je vous prie doresnavent accuser la réception de mes lettres par tel et tel gentilhomme courrier.

Je désire tout contentement à M. de ⟦Marilac⟧, mais je ne croy pas que ⟦{le Roi}⟧ pense à l'affaire dont vous
m'escrivez, que tout le ⟦conseil⟧ ne soit ⟦rejoint⟧, et lors ⟦{le garde des sceaux}⟧ en sera solliciteur.

Vous ne m'avez point mandé ce que vous avez fait touchant la lettre que je vous ay escrite pour M. ⟦de Brassac⟧.
Doresnavant je vous prie me faire response de point en point à ce qui concernera les affaires.

## CXLVII — fr. 3828 no. 10 (Béthune 9322 fol. 30), to Rancé, Susa, 20 April [1629]

J'ay receu vos lettres des 9e et 11e de ce mois. M. ⟦de Brassac⟧ se préparera pour son ⟦ambassade⟧ et sans esventer
encore ceste affaire, il faut qu'il parte au commencement de ⟦[?septembre]⟧ pour arriver à ⟦la fin à Rome⟧. La grâce
que ⟦{le Roi}⟧ luy fait n'empeschera pas l'autre qu'il désire. …

Je désire grandement pour le service de ⟦{le Roi} et {la Reine mère}⟧ que l'affaire de ⟦{Monsieur} s'acommode⟧. Mais
il est impossible de donner des conseils précis et particuliers de loing. …

De Suze, ce 20 apvril.

## CLIII — fr. 3828 no. 17 (Béthune 9322 fol. 48), to the Queen Mother, Susa, 29 April 1629

(pp. 291–292: the state of the troops, in clear, no cipher.) … Plusieurs justes soubçons qu'on a de ⟦l'humeur de
Savoie⟧ ont donné lieu au roy de me laisser icy avec son armée. Il estoit à craindre que, ⟦amusant {le Roi} ici, il⟧
voulust luy laisser ⟦perdre le temps de⟧ réduire les rebelles de Languedoc.

Sa Majesté estant arrivée à Valence, fait estat d'aller assiéger ⟦Privas⟧, et prendre ensuite force petites places
⟦des Sevenes⟧.

Au mesme temps elle prétend faire faire ⟦la dégast de {Montauban} par {M. le Prince}⟧, qui a charge d'y aller par
⟦Bordeaus⟧ et de prendre ⟦{le duc d'Épernon}⟧ en passant; celuy ⟦de {Castres}⟧ par ⟦Estrée et de {Nismes}⟧ par
⟦{Montmorency}⟧.

⟦La pais de {l'Angleterre} est faite⟧, dont il ne faut point parler, s'il vous plaist, que ⟦le [?20] mai ne⟧ soit
passé; c'est la plus grande ⟦afaire⟧ que ⟦{le Roi}⟧ peust ⟦faire⟧, qui donnera un grand ⟦coup aus huguenots et⟧
empeschera ⟦{l'Espagne} et⟧ ceux qui voudroient ⟦brouiler de rien⟧ faire. On a descouvert que ⟦Savoie⟧ vouloit
⟦l'empecher⟧; mais en cela ses efforts ont esté vains. C'est tout ce que je puis mander à Vostre Majesté; …

De Suze, le 29e apvril 1629. Vostre Majesté fera, s'il luy plaist, déchifrer ceste lettre par M. de Rancé.

## CLIV — fr. 3828 no. 18 (Béthune 9322 fol. 51), to Rancé, Susa, 30 April 1629

Monsieur, Je n'ay sceu en aucune façon aucune chose de ce que le sieur de ⟦Nogent⟧ a fait touchant l'affaire de
⟦{Monsieur}⟧; … Nous n'avons rien sceu aussy de la façon avec laquelle l'abolition de M. ⟦de Vendome⟧ a passé, …

⟦La pais de {l'Angleterre}⟧ est ⟦signée⟧ de part et d'autre et la ⟦publication⟧ se fera ⟦le [?20] mai⟧. Et, pour cet
effet, l'ambassadeur de ⟦Venise⟧, nommé ⟦Contarini⟧, qui est résident en ⟦{Angleterre}⟧, escrira à ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧,
entre cy et le dit jour, qu'il a ⟦les articles signés de {l'Angleterre}⟧ entre les mains.

Quand vous aurez receu ceste lettre, ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ ne manquera, s'il luy plaist, de faire solennellement ⟦publier
la pais à Paris⟧; et aussitost il sera besoin de faire une expédition pour faire le mesme par toute ⟦la France⟧.

Il faudra envoier aussy de ma part en tous les ports des provinces maritimes, pour donner le mesme advis; et que
doresnavant ils reçoivent ⟦les {Anglois}⟧ comme les François.

Aussytost la présente receue, vous manderez à Calais, Bologne et le Havre qu'on s'abstienne de faire ⟦mal aus
{Anglois}⟧.

Le mesme a esté desjà fait ⟦en {Angleterre}⟧.

Par accord entre les 2 ⟦courones⟧, 2 ambassadeurs extraordinaires doivent ⟦passer le⟧ 1 ⟦juin⟧. ⟦{L'Angleterre}⟧
demande ⟦{Mme de Chevreuse}⟧; nous n'avons garde de le faire pour les raisons que sçait ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧. Nous avons
mandé que nous avons besoin d'envoier un homme qui entendist les ⟦afaires⟧; je croy que ⟦Chasteauneuf⟧ fera bien ceste
affaire. Mais ce n'est pas chose encore résolue, mais ⟦ici⟧ nous n'en voions aucun autre. ⟦{La Reine mère}⟧ trouvera
bon, s'il luy plaist, que ceste occasion si pressée n'ait pas permis à ⟦{le Roi}⟧ de sçavoir son advis, … De Suze, ce
30 apvril 1629.

## CLV — fr. 3828 no. 19 (Béthune 9322 fol. 54), to Rancé, Susa, 1 May 1629

Depuis vous avoir escrit ⟦la pais de {l'Angleterre}⟧, M. l'ambassadeur de ⟦Venise⟧ estant en doute si
⟦{l'Angleterre}⟧ voudra point 2 ⟦ambassadeeurs, un grand et un⟧ autre, en envoiant 2 ⟦en contrechange⟧.

J'ay creu qu'il estoit à propos que vous sceussiez que si ⟦{l'Angleterre} en veut⟧ 2, l'intention de ⟦{le Roi}⟧ est
d'envoyer MM. ⟦d'Elbeuf et Chasteauneuf⟧. C'est pourquoy si ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ reçoit nouvelles ⟦de Contarini de
{l'Angleterre}⟧ qu'on ⟦en [?veuille]⟧ 2, ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ advertira, s'il luy plaist, promptement ledit sieur
⟦d'Elbeuf⟧ qu'il ⟦parte incontinant⟧ pour ⟦aler en {l'Angleterre}⟧.

Cependant ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ sçaura aussy, s'il luy plaist, que le courrier qui doi(t) partir ⟦de {l'Angleterre}
le⟧ 1 ⟦mai⟧ luy apportera nouvelles si le dit sieur ⟦Contarini⟧ aura ⟦les [?articles] signés de la pais et⟧ s'il faut
⟦la publier à Paris le [?20] mai⟧.

De Suze, le 1er may 1629.

## CLVIII — fr. 3828 no. 21 (Béthune 9322 fol. 59), to Rancé, Susa, 2 May 1629

La douairière ⟦de {la Lorraine}⟧ n'a aucun droit, pas seulement apparent, sur ⟦le Monferat⟧. L'investiture de ce
⟦duché⟧ préfère les masles à toutes femelles, et si les femmes y venoient, celle qu'a espousée ⟦le fils de
{M. de Mantoue}⟧ est préférable à toute autre; de façon que non seulement ⟦{M. de Mantoue}⟧ d'à présent, mais sa
⟦belle-fille⟧ excluent Madame ⟦de {la Lorraine}⟧ tout à fait.

M. ⟦de Piémont⟧ prie ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ de dire ⟦à la Bernet⟧, quand elle partira de Paris, qu'elle se gouverne avec
⟦sa femme⟧ selon ⟦qu'il⟧ voudra. Il est de besoing que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ donne charge à ⟦la Bernet⟧ de dire ⟦au
prince⟧ qu'elle luy a commandé cela. Et cependant luy commander, en effet, de servir ⟦sa [?maistresse]⟧ comme elle
doibt.

⟦{Monsieur}⟧ a tant mandé de fois ⟦le Coudrai et⟧ autres, que luy et quelques autres ⟦s'en sont alés⟧. Il est un peu
fascheux de ne sçavoir chose quelconque de ce qui se passe en ceste affaire, et de ce que ⟦Nogent⟧ a fait.

Hastez, s'il vous plaist, les fontes de Dupré, à qui j'escrits sur ce subjet.

Vous ferez bailler 900# ⟦à Scot et⟧ 500# ⟦à Dumoulin⟧.

De Suze, 2e may 1629.

## CLXVII — fr. 3828 no. 26 (Béthune 9322 fol. 73), to Rancé, 25 May 1629

J'ay escrit plusieurs fois à ⟦{le cardinal de Bérulle}⟧ que de loin je ne donnois point de conseils. J'ay escrit aussy
qu'en général ma pensée estoit que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ devoit ⟦s'adoucir vers {Monsieur}⟧, et qu'estant son ⟦fils⟧,
comme il estoit, ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ s'y porteroit par son bon naturel, … qu'il fallust ⟦délivrer {princesse Marie} et
{Mme de Longueville}⟧. Si j'eusse donné cet advis, j'eusse mandé aussy qu'il le falloit faire à condition que
⟦{Monsieur} se déportast du mariage et que {princesse Marie} s'en alast⟧.

Si quelqu'un dict le contraire (ce que je ne croy pas), je suplie ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ de se faire monstrer mes lettres.

Cependant voiant que peut-estre m'imputeroit-on les mauvais événemens [d'une affaire] en laquelle on peut voir plus
clair que moy, et en laquelle peut-estre n'auroit-on pas en ⟦{Calori} la créance⟧ qu'il mérite, ce que je ne puis
croire de la ⟦bonté de {la Reine mère}⟧, quoy qu'on mande ici de divers lieux, je suplie Nestor de n'avoir aucun esgard
à ce que j'ay escrit par ⟦Saint-Germain⟧ pour le particulier de ceste affaire.

J'avoue que si ⟦{card. de La Valette}⟧ ne désire le contentement de ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ en ce ⟦mariage⟧, je serois
absolument trompé, …

## Notes

- Avenel's footnote decipherments agree with the key everywhere except: CXXXV *mécontent* (Avenel "Mécontentement");
  CLIII *la dégast* (42 = la; Avenel "Le dégast"); CLV *en veule* (Avenel "En veuille"); CXLVII *septembra* (Avenel
  "Septembre"). Where Avenel's note stops with "…" (CXIX nn. 3, 5, 7, 11; p. 235 nn. 2, 4), the cipher run is covered
  by what he gives; the rest of the sentence is in clear.
- *des Sevenes* (CLIII): 40 ~25 27 26 35 22 35 25 = de + ss + evenes; the doubled s spans *des* and *Sevenes*.
- *ambassadeeurs* (CLV): the figures give ambassa + 40 (de) + 30 (e) + urs; printed as the figures spell it, as in
  the 1630 reading (CLVI), and counted as read: the word is there, with one e too many (40 used as a syllable, `key.md`).
- *près d'ele* (CXLVI): the figures give pres + 40 (de) + le, one l; printed as spelled, counted as read.
- *les [?articles] signés* (CLV): one s (25) serves the end of *articles* and the start of *signés* (also on the page
  image of Avenel p. 297); the plural is not in the figures, so *articles* is a hole (23 Nov 2026) and CLV is read in
  part, 53/56.
- Avenel CXXXV n. 4: 60 "signifie toujours Mme de Longueville", but here perhaps M. de Longueville (who receives the
  courier); the table value is kept.
- CLIII n. 10: 62 = Montmorency, though Ventadour made the *dégât* of Castres (Avenel).
Translation

fr3828_en.md

# Richelieu to M. de Rancé (and to the Queen Mother), Feb–May 1629 (BnF fr. 3828, 3825, 3835): English

Translation of `fr3828_reading.md` (Avenel 1858, nos. CXIX–CLXVII, every cipher run checked with the fr. 3829 key).
Ciphered words between ⟦ ⟧; `{...}` = nomenclator figures; `[?x]` = the same holes as in the reading. The clear text
is abridged (…) as in the transcription. Translation by Claude Opus 5.5, close to the French.

## CXIX — fr. 3835 no. 15, to Rancé, [15 (?) February 1629]

… His Majesty wishes the Queen to tell him this news and to give him the brevet ⟦binding⟧ the word ⟦of {the Cardinal
de Bérulle}⟧ that he ⟦will not unite⟧ any ⟦benefice in his gift to his congregation⟧. ⟦{The King}⟧ wishes this of his
own accord.

His Majesty gives La Valasse to M. de Metz, and Saint-Faron of Meaux to M. de Moret. …

The letter that ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ wrote ⟦to the Parlement⟧ is put in terms not well chosen, in my opinion; I fear
it may have ⟦a bad effect⟧ for ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧, and it would have been better to spare ⟦her authority and⟧ the
goodwill of ⟦that company towards {the Queen Mother}⟧. Speak of it only to ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧. ⟦{The King}⟧ may well
write ⟦so⟧, but it was not fitting that ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ ⟦should use⟧ such terms.

As for ⟦Saint-Malo⟧, since ⟦{the Keeper of the Seals}⟧ asks for it, I think ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ should grant it to
him. ⟦{The Queen Mother}⟧ does me more honour than I deserve in wishing for my advice; ⟦La Racinière⟧, an old servant,
would well deserve a pension of ⟦a thousand livres⟧.

⟦{The Queen Mother}⟧ may allow ⟦Madame de Vendôme⟧ to go ⟦and sleep at the Bois de Vincennes⟧ more often, since she
wishes it.

## CXXXV — fr. 3825 no. 28, to Rancé, Susa, 21 March [1629]

… I am too anxious that ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ be told of everything that happens … Last night ⟦{Mme de Longueville}⟧
received a courier, who came riding his horses to death, saying that ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ has had ⟦the {Mme de
Longueville} and {Princess Marie} put in the Bois de Vincennes⟧. We are waiting for the lame man before we believe this
news … ⟦{The Queen Mother}⟧ will surely, I am sure, have seen at the same time to ⟦softening the mind of {Monsieur}⟧.

… Before learning the news brought by the courier of ⟦{Mme de Longueville}⟧, which we do not yet believe, I was in
doubt whether ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ should have the Te Deum sung … to stifle with this joy the fears that might arise
here ⟦discontented⟧. Meanwhile one must speak of the King's victories with ⟦modesty⟧ so as ⟦not to provoke⟧ ⟦{Spain}⟧
beyond measure.

From Susa, 21 March.

## CXLVI — fr. 3828 no. 8, to Rancé, 14 April 1629

I am extremely sorry for the headache of ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧. … The honour I have long had of being ⟦near her⟧ makes
me think that the displeasure she has at the affair of ⟦{Monsieur}⟧ is partly the cause of her illness; I wish with all
my heart that this affair were ended to the satisfaction of Lysandre [the Queen Mother]; one must try to end it;
⟦the people of {Monsieur}⟧ will now see that ⟦this marriage⟧ is no longer a matter he can think of, and so they will
be readier ⟦to bring him to⟧ satisfy ⟦{the King} and {the Queen Mother}⟧.

I did not know for certain of the pregnancy of ⟦the {England}⟧ [Avenel: the Queen of England]. If ⟦{the Queen
Mother}⟧ sends there, as I think she may, it will be right to send a man ⟦capable in affairs, of acting and
speaking⟧. I think the sieur ⟦de Meaux⟧, who has been there before, would be fitter than another. Besides, I think
⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ should wait until the beginning of next month, by which time we shall know how things stand for
⟦peace or war⟧; and between now and then I shall send you a short instruction for ⟦the said de Meaux⟧.

I beg you from now on to acknowledge receipt of my letters by such and such a gentleman courier.

I wish M. de ⟦Marillac⟧ every satisfaction, but I do not think ⟦{the King}⟧ will consider the matter you write of
until the whole ⟦council⟧ is ⟦together again⟧, and then ⟦{the Keeper of the Seals}⟧ will press it.

You have not told me what you did about the letter I wrote to you for M. ⟦de Brassac⟧. From now on I beg you to answer
point by point on whatever concerns business.

## CXLVII — fr. 3828 no. 10, to Rancé, Susa, 20 April [1629]

I have received your letters of the 9th and 11th of this month. M. ⟦de Brassac⟧ will prepare for his ⟦embassy⟧ and,
without yet letting the matter out, he must leave at the beginning of ⟦[?September]⟧ so as to arrive ⟦at the end at
Rome⟧. The favour ⟦{the King}⟧ does him will not stand in the way of the other he wants. …

I greatly wish, for the service of ⟦{the King} and {the Queen Mother}⟧, that the affair of ⟦{Monsieur} be settled⟧.
But it is impossible to give precise and particular advice from afar. …

From Susa, 20 April.

## CLIII — fr. 3828 no. 17, to the Queen Mother, Susa, 29 April 1629

(pp. 291–292: the state of the troops, in clear, no cipher.) … Several well-founded suspicions of ⟦the temper of
Savoy⟧ have led the King to leave me here with his army. It was to be feared that, ⟦keeping {the King} occupied here,
he⟧ might want to make him ⟦lose the time for⟧ subduing the rebels of Languedoc.

His Majesty, having arrived at Valence, intends to besiege ⟦Privas⟧ and then to take a number of small places ⟦of the
Cévennes⟧.

At the same time he means to have ⟦the devastation of {Montauban} made by {Monsieur le Prince}⟧, who is ordered to go
there by ⟦Bordeaux⟧ and to take ⟦{the Duc d'Épernon}⟧ on the way; that ⟦of {Castres}⟧ by ⟦Estrées, and of
{Nîmes}⟧ by ⟦{Montmorency}⟧.

⟦The peace with {England} is made⟧, of which, if you please, nothing must be said until ⟦the [?20] May⟧ is past; it is
the greatest ⟦affair⟧ that ⟦{the King}⟧ could ⟦do⟧, which will deal a great ⟦blow to the Huguenots and⟧ will prevent
⟦{Spain} and⟧ those who would like ⟦to stir trouble from doing anything⟧. It has been found that ⟦Savoy⟧ wanted ⟦to
prevent it⟧; but in this its efforts were in vain. That is all I can send Your Majesty; …

From Susa, 29 April 1629. Your Majesty will, if you please, have this letter deciphered by M. de Rancé.

## CLIV — fr. 3828 no. 18, to Rancé, Susa, 30 April 1629

Monsieur, I have learnt nothing whatever of what the sieur de ⟦Nogent⟧ has done about the affair of ⟦{Monsieur}⟧; … Nor
have we learnt how the pardon of M. ⟦de Vendôme⟧ went through, …

⟦The peace with {England}⟧ is ⟦signed⟧ on both sides and the ⟦proclamation⟧ will be made ⟦on the [?20] May⟧. For this
purpose the ambassador of ⟦Venice⟧, named ⟦Contarini⟧, who resides in ⟦{England}⟧, will write to ⟦{the Queen
Mother}⟧ before that day that he has ⟦the signed articles of {England}⟧ in hand.

When you have received this letter, ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ will not fail, if she pleases, to have ⟦the peace proclaimed
in Paris⟧ solemnly; and at once a dispatch must be made to do the same throughout ⟦France⟧.

Word must also be sent on my behalf to all the ports of the maritime provinces, to give the same notice; and that from
now on they receive ⟦the {English}⟧ like the French.

As soon as this is received, you will send word to Calais, Boulogne and Le Havre that no ⟦harm⟧ be done ⟦to the
{English}⟧.

The same has already been done ⟦in {England}⟧.

By agreement between the two ⟦crowns⟧, two ambassadors extraordinary are to ⟦cross on the⟧ 1st of ⟦June⟧.
⟦{England}⟧ asks for ⟦{Mme de Chevreuse}⟧; we shall take good care not to do so, for the reasons ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧
knows. We have sent word that we need to send a man who understands ⟦affairs⟧; I think ⟦Châteauneuf⟧ will do this
business well. But it is not yet settled, though ⟦here⟧ we see no one else. ⟦{The Queen Mother}⟧ will, if she
pleases, accept that so pressing an occasion has not allowed ⟦{the King}⟧ to learn her opinion, … From Susa, 30 April
1629.

## CLV — fr. 3828 no. 19, to Rancé, Susa, 1 May 1629

Since writing to you ⟦the peace with {England}⟧, the ambassador of ⟦Venice⟧, being in doubt whether ⟦{England}⟧ will
not want two ⟦ambassadors, one great and one⟧ other, sending two ⟦in exchange⟧.

I thought it right that you should know that if ⟦{England} wants⟧ two, the intention of ⟦{the King}⟧ is to send
MM. ⟦d'Elbeuf and Châteauneuf⟧. That is why, if ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ receives news ⟦from Contarini in {England}⟧ that
they ⟦[?want]⟧ two, ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ will, if she pleases, promptly tell the said sieur ⟦d'Elbeuf⟧ to ⟦leave at
once⟧ ⟦to go to {England}⟧.

Meanwhile ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ will also know, if she pleases, that the courier who is to leave ⟦{England} on the⟧ 1st
of ⟦May⟧ will bring her news whether the said sieur ⟦Contarini⟧ has ⟦the signed [?articles] of the peace, and⟧ whether
⟦it is to be proclaimed in Paris on the [?20] May⟧.

From Susa, 1 May 1629.

## CLVIII — fr. 3828 no. 21, to Rancé, Susa, 2 May 1629

The dowager ⟦of {Lorraine}⟧ has no right, not even an apparent one, to ⟦the Montferrat⟧. The investiture of this
⟦duchy⟧ prefers males to all females, and if women came to it, she whom ⟦the son of {M. de Mantoue}⟧ has married is to
be preferred to any other; so that not only ⟦{M. de Mantoue}⟧ of the present, but his ⟦daughter-in-law⟧ exclude
Madame ⟦de {Lorraine}⟧ altogether.

M. ⟦de Piémont⟧ asks ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ to tell ⟦La Bernet⟧, when she leaves Paris, to behave towards ⟦his wife⟧
as ⟦he⟧ shall wish. ⟦{The Queen Mother}⟧ must instruct ⟦La Bernet⟧ to tell ⟦the Prince⟧ that she has ordered her so.
And meanwhile to order her, in fact, to serve ⟦her [?mistress]⟧ as she ought.

⟦{Monsieur}⟧ has sent for ⟦Le Coudray and⟧ others so many times that he and some others ⟦have gone⟧. It is rather
vexing to know nothing at all of what is happening in this affair, and of what ⟦Nogent⟧ has done.

Hurry, if you please, the castings of Dupré, to whom I am writing on the subject.

You will have 900 livres paid ⟦to Scot and⟧ 500 livres ⟦to Dumoulin⟧.

From Susa, 2 May 1629.

## CLXVII — fr. 3828 no. 26, to Rancé, 25 May 1629

I have written several times to ⟦{the Cardinal de Bérulle}⟧ that from afar I gave no advice. I have also written that
in general my thought was that ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ should ⟦soften towards {Monsieur}⟧, and that, he being her ⟦son⟧,
as he was, ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ would be inclined to it by her good nature, … that it was necessary ⟦to release
{Princess Marie} and {Mme de Longueville}⟧. Had I given that advice, I would also have written that it must be done on
condition that ⟦{Monsieur} gave up the marriage and that {Princess Marie} went away⟧.

If anyone says otherwise (which I do not believe), I beg ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ to have my letters shown to her.

Meanwhile, seeing that the bad outcome [of an affair] might perhaps be laid at my door, in which others can see more
clearly than I, and in which perhaps ⟦{Calori}⟧ would not be given ⟦the credit⟧ he deserves (which I cannot believe of
the ⟦goodness of {the Queen Mother}⟧, whatever is written here from various places), I beg Nestor [the Queen Mother]
to take no account of what I wrote by ⟦Saint-Germain⟧ on the particulars of this affair.

I confess that if ⟦{the Cardinal de La Valette}⟧ does not desire the satisfaction of ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ in this
⟦marriage⟧, I should be utterly mistaken, …

fr. 3825 no. 28, 21 Mar [1629], Susa (Avenel no. CXXXV), fr. 3829 key applied read in part

News from Susa a fortnight after the forcing of the pass: a report that the Queen Mother has had Mme de Longueville and Marie de Gonzague put in Vincennes, and advice to speak of the King's victories with modesty so as not to provoke Spain.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (79% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

fr3828_reading.md

# Richelieu to M. de Rancé (and to the Queen Mother), Feb–May 1629 (BnF fr. 3828, 3825, 3835): reading

<!-- measure: cipher_markup=⟦⟧ -->

Nine letters that Avenel prints with their cipher figures in place (Lettres de Richelieu III, 1858, nos. CXIX, CXXXV,
CXLVI, CXLVII, CLIII, CLIV, CLV, CLVIII, CLXVII), read with the fr. 3829 key (`key.md`, with its Nov 2026 extension).
Transcription: `fr3828_avenel_figures.txt`; unedited decryption: `fr3828_decryption.txt`; method and measure:
`fr3828_method.md`. The manuscripts were not seen: everything rests on the figures as Avenel printed them.

Conventions:

- The clear text is Avenel's, as transcribed (abridged with … away from the figures). Words written in cipher are
  between ⟦ ⟧, spelled as the figures spell them; `{...}` inside ⟦ ⟧ = a nomenclator figure, named by the key table.
- Spelling: word division, apostrophes, accents and capitals added; u/v and i/j written by sound (the key has 26 = u/v,
  21 = i/j). Nothing else is normalised: *efet*, *colation*, *Meaus*, *aus*, *afaire*, *aler* are the figures' spelling.
- `[?x]` = a word the figures as printed do not give; the reading follows Avenel's footnote; counted as a hole. The
  five cases: CXLVII *septembre* (last figure 29 = a: *septembra*); "le 20 mai" in CLIII, CLIV and CLV twice (20 is
  the key's d; Avenel takes it as the number); CLV *veuille* (figures *veule*); CLV *articles* (figures
  *articlesignes*, one s for *articles* and *signés*: 23 Nov 2026, review of #813); CLVIII *maistresse* (figures
  *mistrese*; Avenel notes the clerk left out the a).
- Code values taken from Avenel's note at that place rather than the key table (the 19 Nov 2026 rule counts them as
  context values): CLVIII 59 = *M. de Mantoue* twice (table: princesse Marie; Avenel: a slip of the secretary);
  CLIV 84 = *les Anglois* / *Angleterre* (table: l'Angleterre). They are written `{x}` and listed here. Under the strict
  count (owner's decision on #813, 19 Nov 2026) they are holes, like the ten values of the `key.md` Extension where
  they are read (CXIX 68, 69; CXLVI 69; CLIII 55, 61, 62, 95, 96, 97; CLXVII 41, 67, 68): see `fr3828_method.md`.
  The base codes 54 (Monsieur), 59 (princesse Marie) and 60 (Mme de Longueville), named by the key table, are holes
  too in the count (20 Nov 2026): their values come from Avenel's notes to these letters (`key.md`).
- `[d'une affaire]` in CLXVII is Avenel's own supply in the clear text ("il manque ici, dans le manuscrit, un mot").

## CXIX — fr. 3835 no. 15 (Béthune 9329 fol. 46), to Rancé, [15 (?) February 1629]

… Sa Majesté désire que la reyne luy die ceste nouvelle, et luy en baille le brevet ⟦tirant⟧ parole ⟦de
{le cardinal de Bérulle}⟧ qu'il ⟦n'unira⟧ aucun ⟦bénéfice de sa colation à sa congrégation⟧. ⟦{Le Roi}⟧ de son chef
désire cela.

Sa Majesté donne la Valasse à M. de Metz, et St-Faron de Meaux à M. de Moret. …

La lettre que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ a escrite ⟦au parlement⟧ est conçue en termes non bien choisis, à mon advis; je crains
que cela face ⟦un mauvais efet⟧ pour ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧, et il falloit mieux ménager ⟦son autorité et⟧ la bienveillance
de ⟦ceste compagnie vers {la Reine mère}⟧. N'en parlés qu'à ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧. ⟦{Le Roi}⟧ peut bien escrire
⟦ainsi⟧, mais il n'estoit pas à propos que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ ⟦en usast⟧ de la sorte.

Quant à ⟦Saint-Malo⟧, puisque ⟦{le garde des sceaux}⟧ le demande, je crois que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ luy doit accorder.
⟦{La Reine mère}⟧ me fait plus d'honneur que je ne vaux de désirer mon advis; ⟦la Racinière⟧, vieil serviteur,
mériteroit bien une pension de ⟦mil livres⟧.

⟦{La Reine mère}⟧ peut permettre à ⟦Madame de Vendosme⟧ d'aller ⟦coucher au bois de Vincenes⟧ plus souvent
puisqu'elle le désire.

## CXXXV — fr. 3825 no. 28 (Béthune 9319 fol. 77), to Rancé, Susa, 21 March [1629]

… J'ay trop de désir que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ soit adverty de tout ce qui se passe … Hier au soir ⟦{Mme de Longueville}⟧
receut un courrier, qui est venu à tue chevaux, qui dit que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ a fait mettre ⟦la {Mme de Longueville}
et {princesse Marie} au bois de Vincenes⟧. Nous attendons le boiteux pour croire ceste nouvelle … ⟦{La Reine mère}⟧
aura bien, je m'asseure, pourveu à faire ⟦adoucir l'esprit de {Monsieur}⟧ en mesme temps.

… Auparavant que sçavoir la nouvelle qu'a apporté le courrier de ⟦{Mme de Longueville}⟧, que nous ne croions pas
encore, j'estois en doute si ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ devoit faire chanter le Te Deum … pour estoufer par ceste joye les
appréhensions qu'on pourroit prendre ici ⟦mécontent⟧. Cependant, il faut parler des victoires du roy avec
⟦modestie⟧ pour ⟦ne piquer⟧ extraordinairement ⟦{l'Espagne}⟧.

De Suze, ce 21e mars.

## CXLVI — fr. 3828 no. 8 (Béthune 9322 fol. 24), to Rancé, 14 April 1629

Je suis extremement fasché du mal de teste de ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧. … L'honneur qu'il y a long temps que j'ay d'estre
⟦près d'ele⟧ me fait croire que les desplaisirs qu'il a de l'affaire de ⟦{Monsieur}⟧ sont cause en partie de son mal;
je voudrois de bon cœur que ceste affaire fust terminée au contentement de Lysandre, il faut tascher de la terminer;
⟦les gens de {Monsieur}⟧ verront bien maintenant que ⟦ce mariage⟧ n'est plus une affaire à laquelle il puisse penser,
et partant seront-ils plus faciles à ⟦le porter à⟧ donner contentement à ⟦{le Roi} et {la Reine mère}⟧.

Je ne sçavois point asseurément la grossesse de ⟦la {l'Angleterre}⟧. Si ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ y envoie, comme je croy
qu'elle le peust faire, il sera à propos d'y envoyer un homme ⟦capable d'afaires, d'agir et parler⟧. Je croy que le
sieur ⟦de Meaus⟧, qui y a desjà esté, y seroit plus propre qu'un autre. Aussy bien croy-je qu'il est à propos que
⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ attende jusqu'au commencement du mois prochain, auquel temps on sçaura la disposition qu'il y aura
pour ⟦la pais ou la guere⟧; et entre cy et là je vous envoieray une petite instruction pour ⟦ledit de Meaus⟧.

Je vous prie doresnavent accuser la réception de mes lettres par tel et tel gentilhomme courrier.

Je désire tout contentement à M. de ⟦Marilac⟧, mais je ne croy pas que ⟦{le Roi}⟧ pense à l'affaire dont vous
m'escrivez, que tout le ⟦conseil⟧ ne soit ⟦rejoint⟧, et lors ⟦{le garde des sceaux}⟧ en sera solliciteur.

Vous ne m'avez point mandé ce que vous avez fait touchant la lettre que je vous ay escrite pour M. ⟦de Brassac⟧.
Doresnavant je vous prie me faire response de point en point à ce qui concernera les affaires.

## CXLVII — fr. 3828 no. 10 (Béthune 9322 fol. 30), to Rancé, Susa, 20 April [1629]

J'ay receu vos lettres des 9e et 11e de ce mois. M. ⟦de Brassac⟧ se préparera pour son ⟦ambassade⟧ et sans esventer
encore ceste affaire, il faut qu'il parte au commencement de ⟦[?septembre]⟧ pour arriver à ⟦la fin à Rome⟧. La grâce
que ⟦{le Roi}⟧ luy fait n'empeschera pas l'autre qu'il désire. …

Je désire grandement pour le service de ⟦{le Roi} et {la Reine mère}⟧ que l'affaire de ⟦{Monsieur} s'acommode⟧. Mais
il est impossible de donner des conseils précis et particuliers de loing. …

De Suze, ce 20 apvril.

## CLIII — fr. 3828 no. 17 (Béthune 9322 fol. 48), to the Queen Mother, Susa, 29 April 1629

(pp. 291–292: the state of the troops, in clear, no cipher.) … Plusieurs justes soubçons qu'on a de ⟦l'humeur de
Savoie⟧ ont donné lieu au roy de me laisser icy avec son armée. Il estoit à craindre que, ⟦amusant {le Roi} ici, il⟧
voulust luy laisser ⟦perdre le temps de⟧ réduire les rebelles de Languedoc.

Sa Majesté estant arrivée à Valence, fait estat d'aller assiéger ⟦Privas⟧, et prendre ensuite force petites places
⟦des Sevenes⟧.

Au mesme temps elle prétend faire faire ⟦la dégast de {Montauban} par {M. le Prince}⟧, qui a charge d'y aller par
⟦Bordeaus⟧ et de prendre ⟦{le duc d'Épernon}⟧ en passant; celuy ⟦de {Castres}⟧ par ⟦Estrée et de {Nismes}⟧ par
⟦{Montmorency}⟧.

⟦La pais de {l'Angleterre} est faite⟧, dont il ne faut point parler, s'il vous plaist, que ⟦le [?20] mai ne⟧ soit
passé; c'est la plus grande ⟦afaire⟧ que ⟦{le Roi}⟧ peust ⟦faire⟧, qui donnera un grand ⟦coup aus huguenots et⟧
empeschera ⟦{l'Espagne} et⟧ ceux qui voudroient ⟦brouiler de rien⟧ faire. On a descouvert que ⟦Savoie⟧ vouloit
⟦l'empecher⟧; mais en cela ses efforts ont esté vains. C'est tout ce que je puis mander à Vostre Majesté; …

De Suze, le 29e apvril 1629. Vostre Majesté fera, s'il luy plaist, déchifrer ceste lettre par M. de Rancé.

## CLIV — fr. 3828 no. 18 (Béthune 9322 fol. 51), to Rancé, Susa, 30 April 1629

Monsieur, Je n'ay sceu en aucune façon aucune chose de ce que le sieur de ⟦Nogent⟧ a fait touchant l'affaire de
⟦{Monsieur}⟧; … Nous n'avons rien sceu aussy de la façon avec laquelle l'abolition de M. ⟦de Vendome⟧ a passé, …

⟦La pais de {l'Angleterre}⟧ est ⟦signée⟧ de part et d'autre et la ⟦publication⟧ se fera ⟦le [?20] mai⟧. Et, pour cet
effet, l'ambassadeur de ⟦Venise⟧, nommé ⟦Contarini⟧, qui est résident en ⟦{Angleterre}⟧, escrira à ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧,
entre cy et le dit jour, qu'il a ⟦les articles signés de {l'Angleterre}⟧ entre les mains.

Quand vous aurez receu ceste lettre, ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ ne manquera, s'il luy plaist, de faire solennellement ⟦publier
la pais à Paris⟧; et aussitost il sera besoin de faire une expédition pour faire le mesme par toute ⟦la France⟧.

Il faudra envoier aussy de ma part en tous les ports des provinces maritimes, pour donner le mesme advis; et que
doresnavant ils reçoivent ⟦les {Anglois}⟧ comme les François.

Aussytost la présente receue, vous manderez à Calais, Bologne et le Havre qu'on s'abstienne de faire ⟦mal aus
{Anglois}⟧.

Le mesme a esté desjà fait ⟦en {Angleterre}⟧.

Par accord entre les 2 ⟦courones⟧, 2 ambassadeurs extraordinaires doivent ⟦passer le⟧ 1 ⟦juin⟧. ⟦{L'Angleterre}⟧
demande ⟦{Mme de Chevreuse}⟧; nous n'avons garde de le faire pour les raisons que sçait ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧. Nous avons
mandé que nous avons besoin d'envoier un homme qui entendist les ⟦afaires⟧; je croy que ⟦Chasteauneuf⟧ fera bien ceste
affaire. Mais ce n'est pas chose encore résolue, mais ⟦ici⟧ nous n'en voions aucun autre. ⟦{La Reine mère}⟧ trouvera
bon, s'il luy plaist, que ceste occasion si pressée n'ait pas permis à ⟦{le Roi}⟧ de sçavoir son advis, … De Suze, ce
30 apvril 1629.

## CLV — fr. 3828 no. 19 (Béthune 9322 fol. 54), to Rancé, Susa, 1 May 1629

Depuis vous avoir escrit ⟦la pais de {l'Angleterre}⟧, M. l'ambassadeur de ⟦Venise⟧ estant en doute si
⟦{l'Angleterre}⟧ voudra point 2 ⟦ambassadeeurs, un grand et un⟧ autre, en envoiant 2 ⟦en contrechange⟧.

J'ay creu qu'il estoit à propos que vous sceussiez que si ⟦{l'Angleterre} en veut⟧ 2, l'intention de ⟦{le Roi}⟧ est
d'envoyer MM. ⟦d'Elbeuf et Chasteauneuf⟧. C'est pourquoy si ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ reçoit nouvelles ⟦de Contarini de
{l'Angleterre}⟧ qu'on ⟦en [?veuille]⟧ 2, ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ advertira, s'il luy plaist, promptement ledit sieur
⟦d'Elbeuf⟧ qu'il ⟦parte incontinant⟧ pour ⟦aler en {l'Angleterre}⟧.

Cependant ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ sçaura aussy, s'il luy plaist, que le courrier qui doi(t) partir ⟦de {l'Angleterre}
le⟧ 1 ⟦mai⟧ luy apportera nouvelles si le dit sieur ⟦Contarini⟧ aura ⟦les [?articles] signés de la pais et⟧ s'il faut
⟦la publier à Paris le [?20] mai⟧.

De Suze, le 1er may 1629.

## CLVIII — fr. 3828 no. 21 (Béthune 9322 fol. 59), to Rancé, Susa, 2 May 1629

La douairière ⟦de {la Lorraine}⟧ n'a aucun droit, pas seulement apparent, sur ⟦le Monferat⟧. L'investiture de ce
⟦duché⟧ préfère les masles à toutes femelles, et si les femmes y venoient, celle qu'a espousée ⟦le fils de
{M. de Mantoue}⟧ est préférable à toute autre; de façon que non seulement ⟦{M. de Mantoue}⟧ d'à présent, mais sa
⟦belle-fille⟧ excluent Madame ⟦de {la Lorraine}⟧ tout à fait.

M. ⟦de Piémont⟧ prie ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ de dire ⟦à la Bernet⟧, quand elle partira de Paris, qu'elle se gouverne avec
⟦sa femme⟧ selon ⟦qu'il⟧ voudra. Il est de besoing que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ donne charge à ⟦la Bernet⟧ de dire ⟦au
prince⟧ qu'elle luy a commandé cela. Et cependant luy commander, en effet, de servir ⟦sa [?maistresse]⟧ comme elle
doibt.

⟦{Monsieur}⟧ a tant mandé de fois ⟦le Coudrai et⟧ autres, que luy et quelques autres ⟦s'en sont alés⟧. Il est un peu
fascheux de ne sçavoir chose quelconque de ce qui se passe en ceste affaire, et de ce que ⟦Nogent⟧ a fait.

Hastez, s'il vous plaist, les fontes de Dupré, à qui j'escrits sur ce subjet.

Vous ferez bailler 900# ⟦à Scot et⟧ 500# ⟦à Dumoulin⟧.

De Suze, 2e may 1629.

## CLXVII — fr. 3828 no. 26 (Béthune 9322 fol. 73), to Rancé, 25 May 1629

J'ay escrit plusieurs fois à ⟦{le cardinal de Bérulle}⟧ que de loin je ne donnois point de conseils. J'ay escrit aussy
qu'en général ma pensée estoit que ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ devoit ⟦s'adoucir vers {Monsieur}⟧, et qu'estant son ⟦fils⟧,
comme il estoit, ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ s'y porteroit par son bon naturel, … qu'il fallust ⟦délivrer {princesse Marie} et
{Mme de Longueville}⟧. Si j'eusse donné cet advis, j'eusse mandé aussy qu'il le falloit faire à condition que
⟦{Monsieur} se déportast du mariage et que {princesse Marie} s'en alast⟧.

Si quelqu'un dict le contraire (ce que je ne croy pas), je suplie ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ de se faire monstrer mes lettres.

Cependant voiant que peut-estre m'imputeroit-on les mauvais événemens [d'une affaire] en laquelle on peut voir plus
clair que moy, et en laquelle peut-estre n'auroit-on pas en ⟦{Calori} la créance⟧ qu'il mérite, ce que je ne puis
croire de la ⟦bonté de {la Reine mère}⟧, quoy qu'on mande ici de divers lieux, je suplie Nestor de n'avoir aucun esgard
à ce que j'ay escrit par ⟦Saint-Germain⟧ pour le particulier de ceste affaire.

J'avoue que si ⟦{card. de La Valette}⟧ ne désire le contentement de ⟦{la Reine mère}⟧ en ce ⟦mariage⟧, je serois
absolument trompé, …

## Notes

- Avenel's footnote decipherments agree with the key everywhere except: CXXXV *mécontent* (Avenel "Mécontentement");
  CLIII *la dégast* (42 = la; Avenel "Le dégast"); CLV *en veule* (Avenel "En veuille"); CXLVII *septembra* (Avenel
  "Septembre"). Where Avenel's note stops with "…" (CXIX nn. 3, 5, 7, 11; p. 235 nn. 2, 4), the cipher run is covered
  by what he gives; the rest of the sentence is in clear.
- *des Sevenes* (CLIII): 40 ~25 27 26 35 22 35 25 = de + ss + evenes; the doubled s spans *des* and *Sevenes*.
- *ambassadeeurs* (CLV): the figures give ambassa + 40 (de) + 30 (e) + urs; printed as the figures spell it, as in
  the 1630 reading (CLVI), and counted as read: the word is there, with one e too many (40 used as a syllable, `key.md`).
- *près d'ele* (CXLVI): the figures give pres + 40 (de) + le, one l; printed as spelled, counted as read.
- *les [?articles] signés* (CLV): one s (25) serves the end of *articles* and the start of *signés* (also on the page
  image of Avenel p. 297); the plural is not in the figures, so *articles* is a hole (23 Nov 2026) and CLV is read in
  part, 53/56.
- Avenel CXXXV n. 4: 60 "signifie toujours Mme de Longueville", but here perhaps M. de Longueville (who receives the
  courier); the table value is kept.
- CLIII n. 10: 62 = Montmorency, though Ventadour made the *dégât* of Castres (Avenel).
Translation

fr3828_en.md

# Richelieu to M. de Rancé (and to the Queen Mother), Feb–May 1629 (BnF fr. 3828, 3825, 3835): English

Translation of `fr3828_reading.md` (Avenel 1858, nos. CXIX–CLXVII, every cipher run checked with the fr. 3829 key).
Ciphered words between ⟦ ⟧; `{...}` = nomenclator figures; `[?x]` = the same holes as in the reading. The clear text
is abridged (…) as in the transcription. Translation by Claude Opus 5.5, close to the French.

## CXIX — fr. 3835 no. 15, to Rancé, [15 (?) February 1629]

… His Majesty wishes the Queen to tell him this news and to give him the brevet ⟦binding⟧ the word ⟦of {the Cardinal
de Bérulle}⟧ that he ⟦will not unite⟧ any ⟦benefice in his gift to his congregation⟧. ⟦{The King}⟧ wishes this of his
own accord.

His Majesty gives La Valasse to M. de Metz, and Saint-Faron of Meaux to M. de Moret. …

The letter that ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ wrote ⟦to the Parlement⟧ is put in terms not well chosen, in my opinion; I fear
it may have ⟦a bad effect⟧ for ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧, and it would have been better to spare ⟦her authority and⟧ the
goodwill of ⟦that company towards {the Queen Mother}⟧. Speak of it only to ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧. ⟦{The King}⟧ may well
write ⟦so⟧, but it was not fitting that ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ ⟦should use⟧ such terms.

As for ⟦Saint-Malo⟧, since ⟦{the Keeper of the Seals}⟧ asks for it, I think ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ should grant it to
him. ⟦{The Queen Mother}⟧ does me more honour than I deserve in wishing for my advice; ⟦La Racinière⟧, an old servant,
would well deserve a pension of ⟦a thousand livres⟧.

⟦{The Queen Mother}⟧ may allow ⟦Madame de Vendôme⟧ to go ⟦and sleep at the Bois de Vincennes⟧ more often, since she
wishes it.

## CXXXV — fr. 3825 no. 28, to Rancé, Susa, 21 March [1629]

… I am too anxious that ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ be told of everything that happens … Last night ⟦{Mme de Longueville}⟧
received a courier, who came riding his horses to death, saying that ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ has had ⟦the {Mme de
Longueville} and {Princess Marie} put in the Bois de Vincennes⟧. We are waiting for the lame man before we believe this
news … ⟦{The Queen Mother}⟧ will surely, I am sure, have seen at the same time to ⟦softening the mind of {Monsieur}⟧.

… Before learning the news brought by the courier of ⟦{Mme de Longueville}⟧, which we do not yet believe, I was in
doubt whether ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ should have the Te Deum sung … to stifle with this joy the fears that might arise
here ⟦discontented⟧. Meanwhile one must speak of the King's victories with ⟦modesty⟧ so as ⟦not to provoke⟧ ⟦{Spain}⟧
beyond measure.

From Susa, 21 March.

## CXLVI — fr. 3828 no. 8, to Rancé, 14 April 1629

I am extremely sorry for the headache of ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧. … The honour I have long had of being ⟦near her⟧ makes
me think that the displeasure she has at the affair of ⟦{Monsieur}⟧ is partly the cause of her illness; I wish with all
my heart that this affair were ended to the satisfaction of Lysandre [the Queen Mother]; one must try to end it;
⟦the people of {Monsieur}⟧ will now see that ⟦this marriage⟧ is no longer a matter he can think of, and so they will
be readier ⟦to bring him to⟧ satisfy ⟦{the King} and {the Queen Mother}⟧.

I did not know for certain of the pregnancy of ⟦the {England}⟧ [Avenel: the Queen of England]. If ⟦{the Queen
Mother}⟧ sends there, as I think she may, it will be right to send a man ⟦capable in affairs, of acting and
speaking⟧. I think the sieur ⟦de Meaux⟧, who has been there before, would be fitter than another. Besides, I think
⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ should wait until the beginning of next month, by which time we shall know how things stand for
⟦peace or war⟧; and between now and then I shall send you a short instruction for ⟦the said de Meaux⟧.

I beg you from now on to acknowledge receipt of my letters by such and such a gentleman courier.

I wish M. de ⟦Marillac⟧ every satisfaction, but I do not think ⟦{the King}⟧ will consider the matter you write of
until the whole ⟦council⟧ is ⟦together again⟧, and then ⟦{the Keeper of the Seals}⟧ will press it.

You have not told me what you did about the letter I wrote to you for M. ⟦de Brassac⟧. From now on I beg you to answer
point by point on whatever concerns business.

## CXLVII — fr. 3828 no. 10, to Rancé, Susa, 20 April [1629]

I have received your letters of the 9th and 11th of this month. M. ⟦de Brassac⟧ will prepare for his ⟦embassy⟧ and,
without yet letting the matter out, he must leave at the beginning of ⟦[?September]⟧ so as to arrive ⟦at the end at
Rome⟧. The favour ⟦{the King}⟧ does him will not stand in the way of the other he wants. …

I greatly wish, for the service of ⟦{the King} and {the Queen Mother}⟧, that the affair of ⟦{Monsieur} be settled⟧.
But it is impossible to give precise and particular advice from afar. …

From Susa, 20 April.

## CLIII — fr. 3828 no. 17, to the Queen Mother, Susa, 29 April 1629

(pp. 291–292: the state of the troops, in clear, no cipher.) … Several well-founded suspicions of ⟦the temper of
Savoy⟧ have led the King to leave me here with his army. It was to be feared that, ⟦keeping {the King} occupied here,
he⟧ might want to make him ⟦lose the time for⟧ subduing the rebels of Languedoc.

His Majesty, having arrived at Valence, intends to besiege ⟦Privas⟧ and then to take a number of small places ⟦of the
Cévennes⟧.

At the same time he means to have ⟦the devastation of {Montauban} made by {Monsieur le Prince}⟧, who is ordered to go
there by ⟦Bordeaux⟧ and to take ⟦{the Duc d'Épernon}⟧ on the way; that ⟦of {Castres}⟧ by ⟦Estrées, and of
{Nîmes}⟧ by ⟦{Montmorency}⟧.

⟦The peace with {England} is made⟧, of which, if you please, nothing must be said until ⟦the [?20] May⟧ is past; it is
the greatest ⟦affair⟧ that ⟦{the King}⟧ could ⟦do⟧, which will deal a great ⟦blow to the Huguenots and⟧ will prevent
⟦{Spain} and⟧ those who would like ⟦to stir trouble from doing anything⟧. It has been found that ⟦Savoy⟧ wanted ⟦to
prevent it⟧; but in this its efforts were in vain. That is all I can send Your Majesty; …

From Susa, 29 April 1629. Your Majesty will, if you please, have this letter deciphered by M. de Rancé.

## CLIV — fr. 3828 no. 18, to Rancé, Susa, 30 April 1629

Monsieur, I have learnt nothing whatever of what the sieur de ⟦Nogent⟧ has done about the affair of ⟦{Monsieur}⟧; … Nor
have we learnt how the pardon of M. ⟦de Vendôme⟧ went through, …

⟦The peace with {England}⟧ is ⟦signed⟧ on both sides and the ⟦proclamation⟧ will be made ⟦on the [?20] May⟧. For this
purpose the ambassador of ⟦Venice⟧, named ⟦Contarini⟧, who resides in ⟦{England}⟧, will write to ⟦{the Queen
Mother}⟧ before that day that he has ⟦the signed articles of {England}⟧ in hand.

When you have received this letter, ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ will not fail, if she pleases, to have ⟦the peace proclaimed
in Paris⟧ solemnly; and at once a dispatch must be made to do the same throughout ⟦France⟧.

Word must also be sent on my behalf to all the ports of the maritime provinces, to give the same notice; and that from
now on they receive ⟦the {English}⟧ like the French.

As soon as this is received, you will send word to Calais, Boulogne and Le Havre that no ⟦harm⟧ be done ⟦to the
{English}⟧.

The same has already been done ⟦in {England}⟧.

By agreement between the two ⟦crowns⟧, two ambassadors extraordinary are to ⟦cross on the⟧ 1st of ⟦June⟧.
⟦{England}⟧ asks for ⟦{Mme de Chevreuse}⟧; we shall take good care not to do so, for the reasons ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧
knows. We have sent word that we need to send a man who understands ⟦affairs⟧; I think ⟦Châteauneuf⟧ will do this
business well. But it is not yet settled, though ⟦here⟧ we see no one else. ⟦{The Queen Mother}⟧ will, if she
pleases, accept that so pressing an occasion has not allowed ⟦{the King}⟧ to learn her opinion, … From Susa, 30 April
1629.

## CLV — fr. 3828 no. 19, to Rancé, Susa, 1 May 1629

Since writing to you ⟦the peace with {England}⟧, the ambassador of ⟦Venice⟧, being in doubt whether ⟦{England}⟧ will
not want two ⟦ambassadors, one great and one⟧ other, sending two ⟦in exchange⟧.

I thought it right that you should know that if ⟦{England} wants⟧ two, the intention of ⟦{the King}⟧ is to send
MM. ⟦d'Elbeuf and Châteauneuf⟧. That is why, if ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ receives news ⟦from Contarini in {England}⟧ that
they ⟦[?want]⟧ two, ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ will, if she pleases, promptly tell the said sieur ⟦d'Elbeuf⟧ to ⟦leave at
once⟧ ⟦to go to {England}⟧.

Meanwhile ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ will also know, if she pleases, that the courier who is to leave ⟦{England} on the⟧ 1st
of ⟦May⟧ will bring her news whether the said sieur ⟦Contarini⟧ has ⟦the signed [?articles] of the peace, and⟧ whether
⟦it is to be proclaimed in Paris on the [?20] May⟧.

From Susa, 1 May 1629.

## CLVIII — fr. 3828 no. 21, to Rancé, Susa, 2 May 1629

The dowager ⟦of {Lorraine}⟧ has no right, not even an apparent one, to ⟦the Montferrat⟧. The investiture of this
⟦duchy⟧ prefers males to all females, and if women came to it, she whom ⟦the son of {M. de Mantoue}⟧ has married is to
be preferred to any other; so that not only ⟦{M. de Mantoue}⟧ of the present, but his ⟦daughter-in-law⟧ exclude
Madame ⟦de {Lorraine}⟧ altogether.

M. ⟦de Piémont⟧ asks ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ to tell ⟦La Bernet⟧, when she leaves Paris, to behave towards ⟦his wife⟧
as ⟦he⟧ shall wish. ⟦{The Queen Mother}⟧ must instruct ⟦La Bernet⟧ to tell ⟦the Prince⟧ that she has ordered her so.
And meanwhile to order her, in fact, to serve ⟦her [?mistress]⟧ as she ought.

⟦{Monsieur}⟧ has sent for ⟦Le Coudray and⟧ others so many times that he and some others ⟦have gone⟧. It is rather
vexing to know nothing at all of what is happening in this affair, and of what ⟦Nogent⟧ has done.

Hurry, if you please, the castings of Dupré, to whom I am writing on the subject.

You will have 900 livres paid ⟦to Scot and⟧ 500 livres ⟦to Dumoulin⟧.

From Susa, 2 May 1629.

## CLXVII — fr. 3828 no. 26, to Rancé, 25 May 1629

I have written several times to ⟦{the Cardinal de Bérulle}⟧ that from afar I gave no advice. I have also written that
in general my thought was that ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ should ⟦soften towards {Monsieur}⟧, and that, he being her ⟦son⟧,
as he was, ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ would be inclined to it by her good nature, … that it was necessary ⟦to release
{Princess Marie} and {Mme de Longueville}⟧. Had I given that advice, I would also have written that it must be done on
condition that ⟦{Monsieur} gave up the marriage and that {Princess Marie} went away⟧.

If anyone says otherwise (which I do not believe), I beg ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ to have my letters shown to her.

Meanwhile, seeing that the bad outcome [of an affair] might perhaps be laid at my door, in which others can see more
clearly than I, and in which perhaps ⟦{Calori}⟧ would not be given ⟦the credit⟧ he deserves (which I cannot believe of
the ⟦goodness of {the Queen Mother}⟧, whatever is written here from various places), I beg Nestor [the Queen Mother]
to take no account of what I wrote by ⟦Saint-Germain⟧ on the particulars of this affair.

I confess that if ⟦{the Cardinal de La Valette}⟧ does not desire the satisfaction of ⟦{the Queen Mother}⟧ in this
⟦marriage⟧, I should be utterly mistaken, …

fr. 3828 no. 8, 14 Apr 1629 (Avenel no. CXLVI), fr. 3829 key applied read in part

On Monsieur's marriage plans, which his people must now give up, an envoy to the pregnant Queen of England (the sieur de Meaux), and Louis de Marillac's affair, which must wait for the council and the Keeper of the Seals.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (93% here); the holes are marked in the reading.
